Revisé las 16 transcripciones de la semana 5. La clase pasa de los **SVC**, que buscan una frontera con el mayor margen posible, a los **árboles de decisión**, que aprenden qué pregunta hacer en cada paso. Termina con **bosques aleatorios**, donde varios árboles combinan sus predicciones. Para el foro, elegiría una idea concreta y cerraría con una pregunta que invite a comparar experiencias.

**Mi recomendación: margen frente a reglas.** Conecta los dos bloques de la semana sin convertirse en un resumen de toda la clase:

> This week, I found it interesting that SVCs and decision trees approach classification in such different ways. An SVC looks for a boundary with a wide margin, while a decision tree builds a sequence of questions that we can follow and explain. Both can fit the training data too closely: changing C or gamma can make an SVC boundary more sensitive, and allowing too many splits can make a tree very specific. When comparing these models, how would you decide whether a more complex boundary or a deeper tree is actually helping on unseen data?

Otras dos opciones, si prefieres centrarte en un solo tema:

**Sobre árboles de decisión y entropía**

> The decision tree example helped me understand why the first question matters. A useful split creates smaller groups whose labels are more consistent, so the next questions become easier. What I am still thinking about is when to stop: a tree can keep asking questions until it explains almost every training example, but those very specific rules may not work as well on new data. In practice, would you start by limiting the tree’s depth or by requiring more samples in each leaf? Why?

**Sobre SVC y el kernel**

> The XOR example made the kernel trick easier for me to understand. A straight boundary cannot separate those points in the original space, but changing how the model measures similarity allows a nonlinear boundary. The experiments also showed that increasing gamma can create very tight regions around individual points. How would you tell, from validation results or the decision plot, that the kernel is capturing a useful pattern rather than fitting individual training points?

La primera opción me parece la más fuerte para publicar: muestra que seguiste ambos módulos y deja una pregunta abierta que tus compañeros pueden responder desde los experimentos de la clase. Basé las propuestas en las transcripciones de [SVC](<D:/GitHub/ML NJIT/TranscriptClasses/Week 5/DS675.M07.V07.Experiments with SVCs.txt>), [criterios y profundidad de árboles](<D:/GitHub/ML NJIT/TranscriptClasses/Week 5/DS675.M08.V04. Decision Tree Hyperparameters.txt>) y [bosques aleatorios](<D:/GitHub/ML NJIT/TranscriptClasses/Week 5/DS675.M08.V08. Random Forests.txt>).

## Desplegar

Se utiliza la clave SSH existente del usuario; no se guardan credenciales en este proyecto. El script remoto recibe archivo, SHA-256 y un ID Ãºnico `ds675-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS`.

- Enlace del sitio: `/home/automationfreelancer/htdocs/ds675.automationfreelancer.com`.
- Nginx: `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/ds675.automationfreelancer.com.conf`.
- TLS: certificado de Let's Encrypt emitido con la cuenta Certbot existente; renovaciÃ³n webroot.

```text
export function distance(a,b,p=2){
  const ds=a.map((v,i)=>Math.abs(v-b[i]));
  if(p===Infinity)return Math.max(...ds);
  return ds.reduce((s,v)=>s+v**p,0)**(1/p);
}
export function neighbors(points,q,k,p=2){return points.map((v,i)=>({...v,i,d:distance(v.x,q,p)})).sort((a,b)=>a.d-b.d||a.i-b.i).slice(0,k);}
export function predict(points,q,k,p=2,regression=false,weighted=false){
  let ns=neighbors(points,q,k,p);
  if(weighted&&ns.some(n=>n.d===0))ns=ns.filter(n=>n.d===0);
  const weight=n=>weighted&&n.d>0?1/n.d:1;
  if(regression)return ns.reduce((s,n)=>s+n.y*weight(n),0)/ns.reduce((s,n)=>s+weight(n),0);
  const votes={};ns.forEach(n=>votes[n.y]=(votes[n.y]||0)+weight(n));
  return Number(Object.keys(votes).sort((a,b)=>votes[b]-votes[a]||Number(a)-Number(b))[0]);
}
export function mse(points,a,b){return points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(a*v.x+b-v.y)**2,0)/points.length;}
export function gradient(points,a,b){const n=points.length;return [2/n*points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(a*v.x+b-v.y)*v.x,0),2/n*points.reduce((s,v)=>s+a*v.x+b-v.y,0)];}
export function step(points,a,b,rate){const [ga,gb]=gradient(points,a,b);return [a-rate*ga,b-rate*gb];}
export function r2(points,a,b){const mean=points.reduce((s,v)=>s+v.y,0)/points.length;const total=points.reduce((s,v)=>s+(v.y-mean)**2,0);return total===0?null:1-points.length*mse(points,a,b)/total;}
export const shellFraction=(ratio,d)=>1-ratio**d;
export const sigmoid=z=>1/(1+Math.exp(-z));
export function logLoss(y,p){const q=Math.min(1-1e-15,Math.max(1e-15,p));return -(y*Math.log(q)+(1-y)*Math.log(1-q));}
export function perceptronUpdate(w,b,x,y,rate=1){const z=w.reduce((s,v,i)=>s+v*x[i],b),prediction=z>=0?1:0,error=prediction-y;return {z,prediction,error,w:w.map((v,i)=>v-rate*error*x[i]),b:b-rate*error};}
export function regularizedObjective(w,lambda,type='l2'){const data=(w-3)**2,penalty=lambda*(type==='l1'?Math.abs(w):w*w);return {data,penalty,total:data+penalty};}

```

import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {distance,neighbors,predict,mse,gradient,step,r2,shellFraction,sigmoid,logLoss,perceptronUpdate,regularizedObjective} from '../src/math.js';
import {lessons,quizzes,sources,weeks} from '../src/content.js';
import katex from 'katex';
const close=(a,b)=>assert.ok(Math.abs(a-b)<1e-9,`${a} != ${b}`);
test('Distances reproduce the 3-4-5 triangle and L infinity',()=>{assert.equal(distance([0,0],[3,4],1),7);assert.equal(distance([0,0],[3,4]),5);assert.equal(distance([0,0],[3,4],Infinity),4);});
test('kNN uses strict majority; k=1 never returns a class with zero votes',()=>{const data=[0,0,1,0,1].map((y,i)=>({x:[i],y}));assert.equal(predict(data,[0],5),0);assert.equal(predict(data,[0],1),0);});
test('kNN exact coincidences, vote ties and regression',()=>{const data=[{x:[0],y:0},{x:[0],y:1},{x:[2],y:1}];assert.equal(predict(data,[0],3,2,false,true),0);close(predict(data,[0],3,2,true,true),.5);close(predict([{x:[0],y:8},{x:[1],y:10},{x:[2],y:12}],[1],3,2,true),10);assert.deepEqual(neighbors(data,[0],2).map(n=>n.i),[0,1]);});
test('Regression MSE and R2 have hand-calculated results',()=>{const data=[{x:0,y:1},{x:1,y:3},{x:2,y:5}];close(mse(data,1,2),2/3);close(r2(data,1,2),.75);close(r2(data,0,10),-18.375);assert.equal(r2([{x:0,y:1},{x:1,y:1}],0,1),null);});
test('Analytic gradient matches finite differences; update is simultaneous',()=>{const data=[{x:0,y:1},{x:1,y:3}];assert.deepEqual(gradient(data,0,0),[-3,-4]);const [a,b]=step(data,0,0,.1);close(a,.3);close(b,.4);close(mse(data,a,b),2.825);const h=1e-5,g=gradient(data,.6,.2);for(let i=0;i<2;i++){const plus=i===0?mse(data,.6+h,.2):mse(data,.6,.2+h);const minus=i===0?mse(data,.6-h,.2):mse(data,.6,.2-h);close((plus-minus)/(2*h),g[i]);}});
test('High dimension shell uses volume exponent',()=>{close(shellFraction(.9,10),1-.3486784401);close(shellFraction(.5,3),.875);});
test('Logistic probability and log-loss match hand calculations',()=>{close(sigmoid(0),.5);close(sigmoid(Math.log(4)),.8);close(logLoss(1,.8),-Math.log(.8));close(logLoss(0,.2),-Math.log(.8));});
test('Perceptron update follows the course rule',()=>{assert.deepEqual(perceptronUpdate([0,0],0,[1,2],0,1),{z:0,prediction:1,error:1,w:[-1,-2],b:-1});});
test('Regularization separates data loss, penalty and total',()=>{assert.deepEqual(regularizedObjective(2,.5,'l2'),{data:1,penalty:2,total:3});assert.deepEqual(regularizedObjective(2,.5,'l1'),{data:1,penalty:1,total:2});});
test('All lessons have valid connections, sources, weeks, math and quiz references',()=>{const ids=new Set(lessons.map(l=>l.id));assert.equal(ids.size,lessons.length);for(const l of lessons){assert.ok(weeks.some(w=>w.id===l.week));assert.ok(l.code&&l.exercise&&l.solution&&l.steps.length);l.connections.forEach(id=>assert.ok(ids.has(id)));l.refs.forEach(([id])=>assert.ok(sources[id]));katex.renderToString(l.formula,{throwOnError:true});}assert.equal(new Set(quizzes.map(q=>q.id)).size,quizzes.length);for(const q of quizzes){assert.ok(ids.has(q.lesson));assert.ok(q.answer>=0&&q.answer<q.options.length);}});
